description: "Task list for the design-review eval"
---

# Tasks: Design-Review Eval

**Input**: Design documents from `/specs/014-design-review-eval/` (`spec.md`, `plan.md`, `research.md` (section 9: the plan decisions INT-, SR-, AK-, RUN-, PROV-, CS-, GN-, QA-, VD-, RP- and RF-), `data-model.md`, `contracts/`, `quickstart.md`)

**Prerequisites**: `main` at `be01974`, holding features 001 to 011 and 013: the checks-first pre-run and its tool constants (`prerun.py:144-159, 209-213`, 008), `answer_evidence_batch` and `_restate` (008, 013), the replay, its fixtures and its stored tool results (008), `finding_groups` and the words file (009, 013), the fastener checks and the joint map (010), part roles (013). The owner's three replay recordings and their mapping (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\SwReview\recordings.json`, outside the repository) on the development machine: slices 1 and 2 regenerate the replay fixtures from them (existence checked 2026-09-29, plan RK-1). The paid phases (slices 8 and 9) need an OpenAI or Gemini API key on the paid machine; nothing else does (FR-047) but T221, the Task Pane smoke review on the pilot workstation, which needs the licensed seat and the key the pane stores (owner decision 33A). SOLIDWORKS is never started by the eval or its tests, and one task needs the seat: T221, after slice 6's gate and before any paid run (33A). No recorded package, run folder, real profile, private key or private case file is read by a test or enters the repository; every new fixture and case is fictional; nothing committed names a company, a real design or its number, a vault path, a person or a user name (FR-048, FR-051). Owner question 8 (research section 9.7) is answered: option B, owner decision 30A (2026-09-30): held-out core cases take no answer turn, and every verdict names dropped reasoning as untested by it, the anchors showing it with paired intervals (RF-14; T103). Owner decision 29A: the owner reviews the plan and these tasks together before any implementation. The owner reviewed the plan on 2026-10-06, in CLAUDE.md's SMALL CHANGE form (31A, 32A), and took decisions 33A to 36A (research §1 and §9.14), which these tasks apply: the add-in's page and host tests and a replay of the recorded pane sessions at the gates of slices 1, 2 and 6, and one seat smoke review before any paid run (33A; T041, T072, T220, T221); one atomic writer, the product's three older copies moved onto it (34A; T010, T011); a paid smoke run before the pilot and a live test of one real attempt (35A; T171, T244); `benchmark run --jobs` and the cost gate's projected wall clock (36A; T205, T206, T228, T229, T239, T240).

**Tests**: REQUIRED, and **strictly test-first**: every implementation task follows the test task or tasks that must be written and must fail first, names them in its "Acceptance", and names the regression suites that must stay green. Each contract lists its failing-first tests and marks their slice (`contracts/README.md`); those lists are the test tasks. An existing test a change moves is named in the task that moves it and edited deliberately there, what it asserts unchanged, never loosened (plan decision RF-49); a test that goes red by design is named where it does. Golden, fixture and record regenerations are tasks of their own, each in the commit of the change that moves them (feature 008's decision 3A), never a hand edit. Unknown is never 0 (Principle I): a column the contract makes `null` is asserted `null` with its reason in `not_assessed`, never 0.

**Gates before every commit** (from `reviewer/`): `SWREVIEW_REQUIRE_TOKENIZER=1 uv run pytest -q -p no:warnings -o addopts="" -m "not live"` and `uv run ruff check src tests`; where a task changes the committed ledger or what renders it (compare's columns, `docs/llm-efficiency-options.md`, a committed study or pilot folder), `uv run swreview benchmark compare <every run directory under ../benchmarks/studies> --into ../docs/llm-efficiency-options.md --check` exits 0; where a task regenerates the replay fixtures (slices 1 and 2 only), the replay tests on them (`tests/unit/test_replay_findings.py` and every other test that reads `tests/fixtures/replay/`) pass; where the add-in's pane fixture moves (slice 2's D12 commit, RF-28), and at the gates of slices 1, 2 and 6 (owner decision 33A), from the repository root on Windows `dotnet build extractor/SwReview.sln -c Release --nologo -v q` (zero warnings) and `dotnet test extractor/SwReview.sln -c Release --no-build --nologo`; at those three gates also a replay of the recorded pane sessions, the committed replay fixtures through the replay gate's steps 2 and 3 (013 `contracts/tokens.md` section 5), the pane's behaviour unchanged but for the evidence plan.md allows (33A). The opt-in markers run where their task says: `-m perf` (the report's budget, R-18) and `-m simulation` (the 39-scenario sweep, S-10) before any approval. A slice's gate (plan.md, "Delivery order") passes on `main` before the next slice's first merge. From the pane study's commit (T263) on, the ledger check also names the recorded measurement and the noise-floor record: `uv run swreview benchmark compare <every run directory under ../benchmarks/studies> --pilot <every pilot-measurement.json under ../benchmarks/pilots> --noise-floor <every noise-floor.json under ../benchmarks/studies> --into ../docs/llm-efficiency-options.md --check`; before it, a measurement under `benchmarks/pilots/` matches no committed study and the short form above is complete.

**Organization**: Setup; Foundational (the shared pieces two or more stories build on, one of each: the raw-bytes and canonical digests, the system prompt file named beside the checklist, the atomic writer and the product's three older atomic writes moved onto it (34A), the event-line reader and its stream resolver, the stored-result reader, the set root with its refusal type and its two serialized forms, `as_committed`); then one phase per slice of plan.md's delivery order, each a user story's and each closed by its slice's gate task: US1 the scorer (slice 1, MVP); US2 parity (slice 2); US3 key extensions and US4 scripted answers (slice 3, one gate); US5 the generator and validation (slice 4, owner question 8 answered by 30A); US6 the report (slice 5); US7 robustness (slice 6), closed by its gate and then the seat's Task Pane smoke review (`[W]`, 33A); US8 the simulator and verdict (slice 7); US5's sizing, the pilot and cost gate (slice 8, its offline half first, then `[paid]`: the smoke run P0 (35A), then the pilot); Polish (after slice 8's gate); Phase W, the paid pane study and the private slice (slice 9, `[paid]`, no story label).

## Format: `[ID] [P?] [Story] [kind] Lane. Description`

- **[P]**: can run in parallel with the other ready tasks: it touches no file an unfinished task beside it touches, and every task it names as a prerequisite is finished
- **[Story]**: `US1` to `US8`, in the user-story phases only; Setup, Foundational, Polish and Phase W tasks carry none, and neither does T221, the one seat task (Phase W, the paid pane study and the private slice, needs a provider key and never the seat; this feature has no workstation phase, and its one `[W]` task, the Task Pane smoke review of owner decision 33A, follows slice 6's gate)
- **[py]** Python reviewer code, its tests, and the committed data files they read (keys, sets, golden fixtures); **[doc]** documents only; **[regen]** regenerates committed fixtures, goldens or records from code, never by hand; **[gate]** runs the baseline or a slice's gate and records the result, changing no file but this list; **[paid]** needs an OpenAI or Gemini key on the paid machine and spends money; **[W]** needs the licensed SOLIDWORKS seat of the pilot workstation, as feature 013's `[W]` tasks do (`specs/013-engineer-first-review/tasks.md`), and is written into the workstation handover before the sitting
- **Lane S, P, A, G, R, B, V**: the lane that owns the files the task edits in that slice (`plan.md`, "Lanes and file ownership": S scorer, P parity and provenance, A scripted answers, G generator and validation, R report, B robustness, V verdict, simulator and pilot); **Lane I**: integration on `main` (the baseline, the amendment notes, the cross-links, the slice gates, the seat's smoke review), owning no source file (T221's handover documents and its one line in `reviewer/tests/support/seat_tasks.py`, outside every lane's slice, are integration's)
- **Amends NNN**: the other feature's contract the task changes; the task writes that contract's amendment text in the same commit as the code (the one-line note pointing here is T002's)
- **One commit**: tasks that must land together say so, with the order between commits (19A, then origin marks, then D12: RF-2, RF-27, RF-28)

## Path Conventions

- Python: `reviewer/src/swreview/` (the eval's modules under `benchmark/`), tests in `reviewer/tests/`: `unit/`, `integration/` (a test there that needs no native package carries no `integration` marker, RF-26), `golden/`, `perf/` (`-m perf`), `simulation/` (`-m simulation`, new); shared fictional builders in `reviewer/tests/support/`; the case generator in `reviewer/tests/benchmark_cases/` (new)
- Committed cases: `benchmarks/{packages,answer_keys,case_files,sets}/`; paid run folders `benchmarks/pilots/<study folder>/` and `benchmarks/studies/<study folder>/`; the private slice's packages, keys, case files and run folders outside the repository
- Contracts: `specs/014-design-review-eval/contracts/`; amended contracts under `specs/001-agentic-design-review/`, `002-task-pane-assistant/`, `005-llm-efficiency/`, `006-standards-check/`, `008-checks-first-review/`, `010-mechanical-checks/`
- The one file under `extractor/` this feature moves: `extractor/SwReview.AddIn.Tests/Fixtures/review-big-assembly.json` (regenerated by slice 2's D12 commit; no C# change)
- Commands run from `reviewer/` unless the task says otherwise

## Phase 1: Setup

**Purpose**: a measured baseline before any change, and every document that this feature amends or extends saying so.

- [X] T001 [gate] Lane I. Baseline on the feature's worktree at `be01974`, before any change, from `reviewer/`: `uv sync`; `uv run swreview tokenizer fetch` (once per machine; the suite runs with `SWREVIEW_REQUIRE_TOKENIZER=1`, `reviewer/tests/conftest.py:85-106`); `SWREVIEW_REQUIRE_TOKENIZER=1 uv run pytest -q -p no:warnings -o addopts="" -m "not live"` and `uv run ruff check src tests`, both green; from the repository root, `dotnet build extractor/SwReview.sln -c Release --nologo -v q` and `dotnet test extractor/SwReview.sln -c Release --no-build --nologo`, so the re-runs of the add-in's tests, at slice 2's D12 commit over the regenerated pane fixture (RF-28) and at the gates of slices 1, 2 and 6 (owner decision 33A), have a baseline. Record in this line when ticked: the passed, skipped, xfailed and deselected counts and the wall time of the pytest run, ruff's result and the dotnet test counts; `git ls-files --eol config/standards.example.yaml reviewer/src/swreview/agent/checklist_v1.yaml reviewer/src/swreview/agent/prompts/system_v1.md` (this checkout is expected to read `w/crlf`, which slice 2's `.gitattributes` step answers, RK-5); and, existence only, reading nothing inside them, that the owner's recordings mapping and its three mapped folders are present (slice 1's T028 and T031 need them). No file changes; no recording path is written anywhere. **Baseline recorded (worktree at the spec commit over `be01974`, no code change):** pytest 11859 passed, 5 skipped, 0 xfailed, 7 deselected in 431.79 s (7m12s); ruff all checks passed; dotnet build 0 warnings 0 errors; dotnet test Extractor.Tests 9036 passed 0 failed 0 skipped, AddIn.Tests 2129 passed 0 failed 0 skipped (11165 in all); `git ls-files --eol` reads `i/lf w/crlf attr/text` for all three files; recordings mapping present, folders present: big assembly, small-assembly-a, small-assembly-b.

## Phase 2: Foundational (Blocking Prerequisites)

**Purpose**: the pieces two or more stories build on, one of each (plan.md "Phase 0 and Phase 1" points 1, 2 and 6; research section 9: RF-4, RF-29, RF-30, RF-31, RF-48, RF-54, RF-55, RF-56, RF-61, RF-83; owner decision 34A), each test-first, none moving what a review, `benchmark run`, `score` or `compare` writes but one byte class: 34A moves the product's three older atomic writes onto the one writer, and the review's `attention.json`, written in text mode at `be01974`, is then LF on Windows too (T011). A story's own module (the matcher, the key, the question reader, the resolver) stays in the story that defines it, and later stories depend on its task id (Dependencies & Execution Order, at the end). US1 needs T005 and T007 of this phase; each other piece lands before the first story that reads it. The lanes table places these pieces in slices 1 to 4 (S1, P2, S3, G4); they land here, earlier, because no slice's gate moves when they do.

## Phase 3: User Story 1 - The Scorer Credits Only What Was Actually Found (Priority: P1) 🎯 MVP

**Goal**: a finding credits a known defect only on its own check or `x.*` family and on its parts or documents, and for joints, alignments, fits and stacks on their exact references; 13A's status table; correct conditions with ids; eight origins from a step mark and a session record; recall by producer and tag; key format 2 with its load-time refusals and held-out stamps; scorecards versioned and refused across versions; 19A's references on every path.

**Needs**: T005 and T007 of Phase 2.

**Commits, in this order on `main`**: the key's format (T020, T021); the matcher (T022, T023); **19A** (T024 to T028, one commit with every fixture it moves, RF-2); **origin marks** (T029 to T031, one commit, after 19A's, RF-27); origins (T032, T033); the loader and the scorer (T034 to T038, one commit, since the scorer at `be01974` reads the loader T037 replaces); the committed keys (T039, T040). **Purpose**: the answer key grades forbidden findings, expected coverage and questions by code (owner decision 8A): the four extension lists in key format 2 with their load-time refusals (`answer-key.md` rows 9 to 11 and the forbidden halves of rows 6 and 8); forbidden, withdrawn forbidden and tuned-on false alarms, the withdrawn ones read in the scorer's one pass over the event lines through the product's `replay_events` (`scoring.md` 3.3; RF-29, RF-83); expected coverage read through the report's own reader, `goal_readings` (`scoring.md` 4; RF-23); the one question matcher and its columns, request calls read through `read_stored_result` (`questions.md`; RF-6, RF-30); the answer log's record, which these columns read and US4's driver writes; `SCORING_RULES_VERSION = 3` (INT-11).

**Independent Test**: spec.md User Story 3: offline, the key's own expected behaviour scores zero on every new column, and one review per behaviour moves exactly the columns its behaviour defines, by one each. The golden probes over the committed cases are US5's (FR-049, `scoring.md` 9.3); this phase pins each behaviour on sessions built as data and on the oracle of a fictional key holding every list, and scenarios US3-1 to US3-8.

**Needs**: T009, T013 and T015 of Phase 2; US1's T021, T023, T033, T034, T037, T038 and T039; slice 2's gate green on `main` (T072, US2's slice-2 gate).

T079 to T087 land as **one commit ("key extensions, version 3")**. Done 2026-10-08, with one exception approved by the orchestrator, not by the owner: compare's run row reads its findings by origin through `scorecard.final_state_origins`, as the aggregate does, since `finding_origins` now also names the withdrawn forbidden hits, so the ledger's output does not move (research §9.14).

**Checkpoint**: the key grades forbidden findings, expected coverage and questions by code: a forbidden hit is one false alarm and a withdrawn one none; R-5's "checked" is one coverage mismatch naming the item; R-2, R-3, R-6 and R-1 read `questions.md` 7's columns; every column a key does not grade reads "not assessed", never 0; the oracle of a key holding every list scores zero on every column it grades; scorecards are written at `scoring_rules_version` 3. This is US3's half of slice 3's gate (plan.md "Delivery order" row 3); the gate, which US3 and US4 share, is T102 at the end of Phase 6.

---

## Phase 6: User Story 4 - Cases Carry the Engineer's Answers (Priority: P1)

**Purpose**: a case's scripted answers live in a case file, refused before any review when they could never be delivered, and withheld from the reviewer; `benchmark run` plays every package through `drive_review`, which holds the review open and answers its questions through `answer_evidence_batch`, the method the pane's Send reaches, one batch per answer point, Skip as nothing sent, up to `--max-answer-turns` (default 3, in provenance), writing `answer-log.json`; `preflight_set` checks the set and its case files before any review, and `AttemptRequest` carries the case file and the bound (`scripted-answers.md`; `benchmark-run.md` 3 row 4 and 5.1; `provenance.md` 1 and 7's slice-3 halves; `cases.md` 4's case-file halves; RF-13, RF-42, RF-44, RF-61, INT-14).

**Independent Test**: spec.md User Story 4: offline, a case whose script answers the fit question records one answer turn, a scripted re-ask is counted, and the session shows the answer as the pane would (`tests/integration/test_scripted_answers_flow.py`, `tests/unit/test_benchmark_answers_parity.py`; quickstart section 6); scenarios US4-1 to US4-9.

**Needs**: US3's T078 (the answer log's record), T082 (`QuestionIds`, `asks_within`, `is_part_roles_question`) and T087 (the scorer reading the log); T009 and T017 of Phase 2; US2's parity work on `main` (T050 to T052 and T054: US2's `AttemptRequest` with the model view and the profile, `_review_fn` taking `attempt`, provenance schema 2 and the saved set written before any review through `write_atomic`, and `benchmark run`'s refusal rows 1 to 3 and 5 to 7).

**Commits, in this order on `main`**: the case file (T088 to T090); scripted-answer matching (T091, T092); the case file in the set and the guard (T093, T094); the run's case files and bound (T095 to T097); the driver (T098, T099); the driver in `benchmark run` (T100, T101).

## Phase 7: User Story 5 - A Case Set That Can See a 10-Point Drop (Priority: P1)

**Purpose**: a committed generator writes every fictional case (package, key, case file and set entry) from one case spec, through the formats' data-only models, byte for byte and never from check output (FR-027); the committed set reaches the floor, 36 held-out model-produced defects over the nine core designs with one fully clean twin per core case (24A), and represents every anchor R-1 to R-8 and analyst-found defect AF-1 to AF-3 (FR-028, FR-029); `cover-blind-tap` is regenerated with D-1 reachable (FR-010); an offline validation apart from the generator proves each producer tag and each key's reachability, FR-054's two legs plus decoys (SC-014); golden scorecards, their probes and the version record pin the scorer (FR-049, SC-013); sets gain their keys, keyed and keyless forms and refusals (FR-030); `benchmark set-summary` prints the per-family summary (FR-033). Plan slice 4, lane G (`plan.md`, "Delivery order" row 4; "Lanes and file ownership").

**Independent Test**: spec.md User Story 5: offline, SC-001, SC-004, SC-006, SC-011 and SC-014 hold, with this slice's SC-002 and SC-013; quickstart Scenarios 2, 3, 4 and 5; plan.md slice 4's gate (T158).

**Needs**: slice 3's gate green on `main` (T102, closing US3's and US4's phases); T103, done (30A answers owner question 8); Phase 2's T005, T017 and T019; US1's T021, T023, T033, T034, T037, T038 and T039; from US2, `.gitattributes` `eol=lf` on the digested inputs with `test_line_endings.py` green (T057, T058; before `stamps.CURRENT` is written, plan "Sequencing"), `_review_settings` resolving `--pane-defaults` (T044), and `AttemptRequest` carrying the model view and the standards profile (T050); from US3, the four extension lists in `answer_key_model`, `write_key` and the loader (rows 9 to 11; T079, T080, T084), scoring rules version 3 with the forbidden, coverage and question columns (T087), the oracle's coverage rows and request calls in `scoring_probes.py` (T085), and `benchmark/questions.py` (T082); from US4, the data-only `benchmark/case_file.py` (T089), `answers.load_case_file` (T090), `runner.preflight_set` with its `PreflightedSet` (T097), the set's `case_file` and `case_file_sha256` fields (T094), `max_answer_turns` (T097), and `_review_fn` through `drive_review` (T101).

**Commits, in this order on `main`** (every commit passes the full gate, so each failing-first test lands with the task that turns it green): the builders (T104 with T105, T106 with T107, T108 with T109, T110 with T111, a commit each); families (T112, T113); the set file (T114, T115); the existence checks (T116, T117); the set summary (T118, T119); `score`'s optional `--answer-keys` (T120, T121); the ingest inputs moved out of the benchmark folder (T122, T123), before the generator writes, whose stale scan would otherwise name them; the generator's machinery and seed kinds (T124 to T129, one commit); the validation's legs (T130, T131); the case specs (T132 to T144, a commit per design allowed, no committed output moving); **the committed set** (T145 to T150, T151's generated tree and T152, one commit; T145 to T150 are written only after the last design commit, since each stays red until T151); **the golden scorecards and the version record** (T153 to T157, one commit); the gate (T158).

**Design rules** (every case-spec task, T132 to T144, follows them; `generator.md` 2, 3, 5 and 7; `cases.md` 2 and 6):
1. The task fills its stub `reviewer/tests/benchmark_cases/designs/<design>.py` (made by T126) and touches no other design's module: `DESIGN` (its design word, places and build) and `SPECS`, the core cases first and then the anchor cases in `cases.md` 6.3's order; each twin is derived at render (`twin=True`), never written.
2. Places, not ids: every id a seed, entry, answer, correct call or decoy carries is a place name the design defines (component, document, hole, fastener, drawn dimension); the spec never types a check, producer, expected status, withheld value or family its kind fixes.
3. The clean base (5.1 to 5.5; GN-3): `PackageBuilder`; trees and one global equation on every custom part through `features.part_trees`, compliant; a fixed first component; masses consistent with their materials; drawing documents carrying the house card `tests/support/standards.card(profile)`; every name from `config/standards.example.yaml` (5.2: custom parts `EX-1101-01.SLDPRT`-shaped with the revision under `revision.property`, the hygiene part number equal to the stem and a summary unique in the package; bought parts in the vendor file shape, in a folder `part_roles.bought_folder_names` names, with a vendor property), so no part is unclear unless the spec plants the conflict; every screw with `fastener_row=True` (GN-2); every path under `FICTIONAL_ROOT`, `/Designs/` or the example profile's `vault_root`; profile A's names (`tests/support/mechanical.py:108`) never.
4. No tolerance source anywhere in a core design: no toleranced model dimension, model position tolerance, ISO 286 Hole Wizard class or validated drawing binding (`tolerance_source`, `generator.md` 3); limits live only on ingested sheets, through `SheetBuilder`.
5. The drawing-document rule (`cases.md` 6.2; CS-8): fit, stack and tolerance dimensions on the assembly's drawing; a drawing seed on the part's own drawing, which carries no other seed and no condition.
6. A place for every decoy its kinds name: a sibling screw joint, a second hole pair between the same parts, a sibling fit on the assembly sheet, a stack contributor that can be left out.
7. Core cases are `<design>-1` and `<design>-2`, each exactly the two seeds `cases.md` 6.2's table gives it, both `HeldOut(stamps.CURRENT)`, of two judgement families, with `twin=True`, `sets=("committed",)` and no answers (CS-9).
8. Descriptions and drawing texts are the author's English; a drawing text quotes only numbers the drawing shows (`tools/session.py:673`).
9. Acceptance of a design task: for each of its specs and each derived twin, `tests.support.reachability.check_spec(spec)` returns `[]` (leg (a) under pane, (b) under off and pane, decoys under off), each key `render.render_spec(spec)` renders loads through `benchmark/answer_key.load_answer_key` against its rendered package, each rendered case file through `answers.load_case_file` against its package and the example profile, and `spec.check_specs(SPECS)` passes the design's specs (`generator.md` 10's rows 1 to 7 and row 8's per-entry halves; row 8's coverage half, `spec.check_anchor_coverage`, is checked only by `generate.render_all()` over every design, T146 and T151); run it with `uv run python -c "from tests.support.reachability import check_design; print(check_design('<design>'))"`, which prints `[]`. A finding leg (a) names is fixed in the design, never keyed away (5.3; RK-3); a drawing kind code detects is re-tagged or replaced in the spec (RK-4); a change that would move a count of `cases.md` 6.1 or 6.2 stops the task and is taken to the owner.

## Phase 8: User Story 6 - One Report the Owner Reads (Priority: P2)

**Purpose**: slice 5 of plan.md's delivery order. It lands three pieces:
- one offline command, `swreview benchmark report <run dirs...> --out <folder>`, that builds one static page and one transcript page per package run from run folders as `benchmark run` writes them, every number the scorecard's (`contracts/report.md`);
- the record of the system prompt and every user message a benchmark attempt sent, `prompts.json` (`contracts/benchmark-run.md` 12; FR-057; D11; INT-1, RUN-12);
- `benchmark/recording.read_recording` on the product's one event-line reader, its `_events` gone (RF-29).

The report reads each run's stream through `event_stream`, plain or packed (RF-83), and each stored result through `read_stored_result` (RF-30).

**Independent Test**: spec.md User Story 6. Offline, a scripted run of three cases builds a page whose counts match the scorecards, and each row links to a transcript that starts with the recorded system prompt (R-1, T163). Scenario 2 is R-5 and R-15. Scenario 3 is R-6 and R-16. Scenario 1's half that shows both run folders is R-2. plan.md slice 5's gate is T170.

**Needs**:
- T009 (`write_atomic`), T013 (`replay_events` with `on_unreadable`, `event_stream`), T015 (`read_stored_result`) and T017 (`load_saved_set`, `REPO_ROOT` in `benchmark/sets.py`), from Phase 2.
- US1's T033 (origins), T034 (`write_key`) and T038 (`read_scorecard`).
- T050, T051 and T056: US2's saved set written location-free through `saved_form`, its `AttemptRequest` with the model view and the profile, and `tests/support/local_paths.py`'s scanner.
- T086 and T087: US3's one-pass scorer read through `replay_events` and `event_stream`, version 3's columns, and `test_event_lines.py` 4's `score_run` row.
- T078, T090, T097 and T101: US4's `_review_fn` through `drive_review`, its case files and `answer-log.json` with the `AnswerLog` model.
- T115 and T121: US5's set entry fields `key` and `represents`, and keyed-set scoring with no `--answer-keys` (R-8's run).

**Commits, in this order on `main`**:
1. The recording reader (T159, T160).
2. The prompts record (T161, T162). It does not depend on commit 1.
3. The report (T163 to T169, one commit: T163 to T167's tests are red until T169, and T168's shared names land with the code that imports them, so no gate runs with a red test in the working tree).

No fixture, golden or record regenerates in this slice.

**Left to US7** (lane R's task inside slice 6, T216 and T217; its tests are R-3, R-4 and R-19). Each of these reads a scorecard field the robustness slice adds (`run_status`, `cost`, `served_models`, `price_table`, `unscored_packages`, `failed_attempts`; `scoring.md` 7):
- the status and cost columns;
- the head's price table;
- the totals by status, and the line for the cost billed by failed attempts;
- the transcript header's status, responding model and cost;
- `report.md` 5 item 5's failed-attempt pages;
- `test_prompts_record.py`'s abandoned-attempt test.

This slice's page renders none of them and shows none of them as 0.

## Phase 9: User Story 7 - A Run That Fails Says So (Priority: P2, before any paid study)

**Purpose**: slice 6, robustness (plan.md "Delivery order" row 6; `benchmark-run.md` 4 to 15; `provenance.md` 1's clock values and `retry_policy`, 2's served-model check, 6.6's reach over `run-status.json`, the attempt folders and `event-streams.json`, 6.7's `benchmark pack` and 7's robustness fields; `scoring.md` 7's run fields, 8's next version and 9.3's behavioural-stop probe; `report.md` R-3, R-4 and R-19). Every package attempt runs on a daemon worker under a wall clock; request-level retries back off with jitter and are counted exactly; the served model and the provider's own stops are written on every stream (OpenAI's policy codes, RF-76; 26A's overflow, recognised and inferred, RF-77); `run-status.json` records every attempt, a kept attempt is promoted by one rename, and `benchmark resume` re-runs only infrastructure failures; a dated price table prices each round; the harness writes no local path (RF-41); `benchmark pack` compresses a finished run's streams and records their digests (28A, RF-82); the scorer copies outcome, cost and served models into the scorecard under the next scoring-rules version; `benchmark run --jobs N` runs up to N packages at once, recorded and never must-match (owner decision 36A); a live test drives one real attempt end to end, run only on demand (35A); then, after the gate, the seat's Task Pane smoke review (33A). No paid run happens before this phase's gate (FR-036 needs it; plan "Sequencing").

**Independent Test**: spec.md User Story 7, SC-007: offline, a scripted provider that fails on one package, hangs silently on a second and safety-stops a third; the set completes; the first two are counted as failures, in no score, and alone re-run on resume; the third is a behavioural stop with its re-asks and forbidden hits counted (T208; quickstart scenario 8); acceptance scenarios 1 to 7; plan.md slice 6's gate (T220); then the seat's smoke review (T221).

**Needs**: slice 5's gate green on `main` (T170, US6's slice-5 gate), and from the phases before it: T005 (`benchmark/digest.py`), T009 (`write_atomic`), T013 (`replay_events` with `on_unreadable`, `event_stream`, `PACKED_EVENTS_FILE_NAME`), T017 (`REPO_ROOT` in `benchmark/sets.py`, `load_saved_set`); T050 (US2's `AttemptRequest` with the model view and the profile), T051, T052 and T054 (US2's provenance and saved set written before any review, with section 3's rows 1 to 3 and 5 to 7 and the run-start adapter handed to the first attempt, RF-60), T056 (US2's committable-folder scanner `reviewer/tests/support/local_paths.py`), T060 (US2's `display_digest`), T087 (US3's scorer at version 3 with its one pass through `replay_events`), T097 and T099 (US4's `preflight_set`, `drive_review` and `AttemptRequest.case_file` and `.max_answer_turns`), T153 to T157 (US5's golden scorecards, probes, `GOLDEN_EXCLUDED`, `GOLDEN_IDENTITY` and `scoring_rules_versions.json`), T162 (US6's `prompts.json` writer in `_review_fn` and `benchmark/report_page.py`).

**Commits, in this order on `main`**: the live attempt test (T171, alone, skipped in every gate); event bodies (T172, T173); provider errors, served model and stops (T174, T175); retries (T176, T177); the scripted provider's affordances (T178, T179); `sha256_of_bytes` (T180, T181); `without_local_paths` (T182, T183); served aliases and prices (T184 to T186); `attempt_facts` (T187, T188); **the attempt driver** (T189 to T200, one commit: the one stop mechanism, `drive_review`'s required `stop_requested` and its one caller, the worker and the body land together, since each alone leaves the suite red); run options and records (T201, T202, one commit with the handoff allowlist, which `test_the_handoff_allowlist_names_every_provenance_field` holds equal to the record); resume (T203, T204); packages at once (T205, T206, one commit with the handoff allowlist, as T201 and T202); **the scorer's run fields** (T207 to T211, one commit: a new required field bumps `SCORING_RULES_VERSION` in the commit that regenerates the goldens and appends the version row, INT-11, SC-013); compare's served-model re-check (T212, T213); `benchmark pack` (T214, T215); the report's status and cost (T216, T217, lane R); the handoff export (T218, T219); the gate (T220); then, on the seat, T221, before any paid run (33A). A test whose behaviour lands in a later commit is written in that commit's test task, never committed red.

**Contract gaps this phase resolves, for the owner's review of these tasks (29A)**: (1) `benchmark-run.md` 5.1 passes the attempt's listener as the review's `callbacks` and composes `without_local_paths` into its `redact`, but `AttemptRequest` (data-model section 7) carries only `stop` for this slice, so the body can reach neither the listener nor the run folder: T198 adds `listener` and `run_dir` beside `stop` and records both in data-model section 7 and `benchmark-run.md` 5.1 in its commit. (2) Under a retry policy, a Gemini transport error that exhausts its retries would escape as a raw `httpx` class and classify `internal_error`: T177 raises it as a `GeminiProviderError` naming the `httpx` class, so it is `provider_error`. (3) `package_outcome` of a package with no `run-status.json` entry, or whose last attempt has no outcome, is unspecified: T209 refuses it naming `benchmark resume`, as `benchmark pack` refuses an attempt with no outcome. (4) Section 5.2's listener "never raises": T197 records its own exception as an `internal_error` condition and sets the stop, so a listener bug is never silent (Principle I). (5) Section 3 row 6's price entry would refuse the scripted provider, which is never priced (section 13, "synthetic") and which quickstart scenario 7 runs as a pane study: T202 does not apply the row to `--provider fake`.

The inferred overflow is `isinstance(error, GeminiRequestError)`, the class imported from `agent/providers/gemini_provider.py` inside that branch, never at module level, after an answered round; never by message, `error_class` text or provider. `AttemptOutcome` (frozen): `kind`, `class_`, `stop_reason`, `error_class` (`getattr(error, "error_class", type(error).__name__)`), `message` (the one-line message, unredacted here; the driver redacts), `stream_active_at_limit`. ## Phase 10: User Story 8 - The Pane Study Reaches a Verdict Noise Cannot Flip (Priority: P2, before the pane study)

**Goal**: one package-level t interval behind every rule and shown measure; one `judge` read in section 5's order by compare and by the simulator; the simulator's 39 reference scenarios holding both 5% bounds; the pane study routed to its own block with section 7's refusals, the pilot measurement's model and digest, the noise-floor record read and never simulated by compare, and the private slice's row (23A).

**Independent Test**: spec.md User Story 8: offline, SC-010 holds (the simulator prints its inputs and holds both 5% bounds at the size, repetitions and levels it picks, at every reference scenario; quickstart scenario 10), and US8-1 to US8-15 hold on hand-built scorecards (`verdict.md` 12: V-1 to V-27, C-1 to C-17, S-1 to S-9); plan.md slice 7's gate (T236).

**Needs**: (after T220, US7's slice 6 gate): `PackageScore.run_status`, `cost`, `served_models` and `Scorecard.unscored_packages` (INT-9); (after T066 to T069, US2's ledger 1.1 commit, with T044 and T065): `pane_studies` named in the 1.1 schema record, `compare.settings_differed`, `INERT_SETTINGS`, `PANE_STUDY` and `PRIVATE_PANE_STUDY`, the pane studies' run-level checks, raw rows for pane studies; (after T087, US3's version-3 scorer): `forbidden_hit_finding_ids`, `withdrawn_forbidden_hit_finding_ids`, `tuned_on_false_alarm_finding_ids`, coverage and question columns; (after T151, US5's committed set): keyed saved sets with `twin_of` and `case_file`; T005 (`canonical_sha256`), T017 (`sets.REPO_ROOT`, which T234's `compare_runs` root reads), T033 (`origins.MODEL_ORIGINS`), T169 (`report_page.deciding_false_alarm_ids`, which T227 moves beside `MODEL_ORIGINS`).

**Commits, in this order on `main`**: stats (T222, T223); the simulation marker and gate (T224, T225); the rule (T226, T227); the measurement's model (T228, T229); the simulator (T230, T231); the pane block, compare's options and 005's pointer (T232 to T234, **one commit**); the sweep's printout (T235); the gate (T236). No scorecard field moves in this slice, so `SCORING_RULES_VERSION` and the golden scorecards do not move; `LEDGER_SCHEMA` stays `1.1` and the committed header does not move (INT-10).

Also: the reasons are exactly `RECALL LOSS`, `FALSE-ALARM RISE`, `CODE DEFECT LOST`, `CLEARED`, `BELOW FLOOR`, `RECALL NOT COMPUTABLE` and `LOWER END AT OR BELOW -10 POINTS`, suffixed with the figure where the rule has one, as `verdict.md` 8's `"LOWER END AT OR BELOW -10 POINTS: sensitivity -11.2"`; every firing rule is listed with its figure; the verdict is one of `quality loss`, `no 10-point loss`, `inconclusive`; the deciding false alarms are exactly those whose `finding_origins` value is in `origins.MODEL_ORIGINS` and that are not in `tuned_on_false_alarm_finding_ids`, one hitting no entry counting (17A, 20A, D15); the false-alarm rule averages over every package of the set, clean twins and anchors included (FR-044); a package in `unscored_packages` counts in neither figure (D2); `judge` over a `StudyStats` whose leading axis holds three studies returns, for each, what `judge` returns for that study alone (the simulator's path, VD-5); `study_stats` over one arm's runs (the paid pilot's reading, Phase 11) fills the off arm and leaves the on arm empty; `study_stats` reads recall from held-out model-produced defects only, so a tuned-on model-produced defect, detected or missed, moves neither figure's recall (US5-6: a tuned-on item is graded as a regression guard, never in held-out recall); one definition of the deciding false alarms: `report_page.deciding_false_alarm_ids is origins.deciding_false_alarm_ids`, a spy patched on `swreview.benchmark.origins.deciding_false_alarm_ids` sees `study_stats` call it, and `benchmark/verdict.py`'s import statements (read with `ast`) name nothing from `benchmark/report_page.py`. ---

## Phase 11: User Story 5 (Sizing) - The Pilot and the Cost Gate (Priority: P1, before the pane study)

**Goal**: an offline `benchmark pilot` that measures the paid pilot's off-arm runs (FR-055's rates, co-movement, code defects, stops, clearances, false alarms, cost and time), lists what it never detected, sizes the study by simulation and writes an unapproved, digested measurement; `benchmark noise-floor`; then, on the paid machine, once the seat's smoke review has passed (33A), the smoke run P0 (35A: two packages, one repetition per arm, every row read and any wiring fixed before the pilot), the three-repetition pilot (27A), its measurement with the study's projected wall clock at the chosen concurrency (36A), the owner's approval, any approved growth units, and the pilot's packed folders committed in one commit before any study run.

**Independent Test**: spec.md US5-13 and US5-14 (FR-055): offline, P-1 to P-14 and `compare.load_runs`'s tests (T237); on the smoke run and the pilot, SC-008 (no "cost unknown"), and on the pilot SC-005 and the projected wall clock printed (quickstart P0 to P3); plan.md slice 8's gate (T253).

**Needs**: T236; (after T220, US7's slice 6 gate, and, for the paid steps, T221, the seat's smoke review, 33A): the robustness slice, `benchmark run --jobs` (36A), `benchmark resume`, `benchmark pack` and the price table filled from the providers' pages (no paid run before it, FR-036); (after T170, US6's R-18 perf run, recorded before the pilot, RK-11); (after T151 and T158, US5's committed set and its validation); T023 (the matcher and its `targets_overlap`), T037 (the key loader).

**Commits, in this order**: `compare.load_runs` (T237, T238); the pilot's estimators and `benchmark pilot` (T239, T240); the noise floor (T241, T242); the offline half's gate (T243); then on the paid machine, one commit ("the pilot", T244 to T252): the packed smoke and pilot folders, the approved measurement and any grown cases, before any study run (RF-40, RK-15); the gate (T253).

## Phase W: The Paid Pane Study and the Private Slice (slice 9)

**Purpose**: the owner's steps on a machine with a provider key: the pane study's two arms at the approved size, its noise floor, its verdict in the committed document and its packed folders; then the private slice as its own study, outside the repository. No story label; every run step spends money.

**Independent Test**: plan.md slice 9's gate: the verdict renders (or is refused, naming why); FR-051's test over the committed study and pilot folders, their packed streams each matching its recorded digest; the study committed after the last repetition, packed, with its noise-floor record (quickstart P4 and P5).

**Needs**: T253 (the pilot commit with the approved measurement on `main`); every run from that one commit (RK-15).

- [ ] T260 [paid] Lane V. The study runs (quickstart P4; ab-harness section 2; `provenance.md` 3.2 and 4; FR-014, FR-032; RK-15): the paid machine's checkout at exactly T252's commit, tracked tree clean, and no commit made there until T263; the approved repetitions `R`, arms alternated, for `n` in 1 to `R`, from `reviewer/`: `uv run swreview benchmark run --set ../benchmarks/sets/committed.json --out ../benchmarks/studies/pane-openai/off-$n --provider openai --model <model> --effort <effort> --study pane_defaults --arm off --rep $n --no-explain-findings --standards-profile ../config/standards.example.yaml --jobs <N>`, then the same with `--arm on --pane-defaults` into `on-$n`: the model, effort, profile, set and typed explanation setting the paid pilot used (`verdict.md` 10.1), and `<N>` the approved `sizing.jobs` (36A: record-only, so another N refuses nothing, but the approved projection assumes it); `uv run swreview benchmark resume <folder>` on every infrastructure failure; `uv run swreview benchmark score <folder>` for every run. The run folders stay untracked (`tree_dirty` false) until the last repetition is scored. A run-time refusal (dirty tree, unknown commit, a digested input holding a carriage return, an unpriced model) is fixed before any paid run, never worked around. Record in this line the dates, the commit, `R`, per arm and repetition the packages completed, bound-reached, stopped (by class) and re-run, and the total cost.
- [ ] T261 [regen] Lane V. After the last repetition is scored, once (`verdict.md` 9.7; RF-53): `uv run swreview benchmark noise-floor ../benchmarks/studies/pane-openai/{off,on}-* --pilot ../benchmarks/pilots/pane-openai/pilot-measurement.json --out ../benchmarks/studies/pane-openai/noise-floor.json`. Record in this line the points (or "above 50 points" or "not attainable"), the seed and the numpy version. Acceptance: `noise-floor.json` written, and compare over the study reads its state `computed`.
- [ ] T262 [regen] Lane V. The verdict (quickstart P4; `verdict.md` 5, 7, 8; US8-1, US8-8; FR-046): `uv run swreview benchmark compare ../benchmarks/studies/pane-openai/{off,on}-* --pilot ../benchmarks/pilots/pane-openai/pilot-measurement.json --noise-floor ../benchmarks/studies/pane-openai/noise-floor.json --into ../docs/llm-efficiency-options.md`; the same folders compared twice with `--out` into two temporary folders give identical `ledger.json` bytes. The pane block names the verdict with its reasons, both paired rules on both figures with their intervals and `alpha`, both guards, the drops, the approval line, the noise floor with its seed and numpy version, the settings the arms differed in (dropped reasoning among those the verdict does not test while no held-out case takes an answer turn), and the shown table; the pilot's runs render as raw rows marked `off (pilot)`. A refused verdict (exit 1, the raw rows still written) is the study's result: record the refusal and report it to the owner, never work around it. Record in this line the verdict and its reasons. Acceptance: the pane block renders (or its refusal is recorded), and the two `--out` renders are byte-identical.
- [ ] T263 [regen] Lane V. Pack the study's folders after their last resume (quickstart P4; `provenance.md` 6.7; 28A; RK-17): `uv run swreview benchmark pack ../benchmarks/studies/pane-openai/{off,on}-*`, as T252 packs (packing moves neither `noise-floor.json` nor the ledger: both read scorecards, saved sets and sessions only); `git status` lists every packed stream and no plain one; `test_committable_paths.py` passes over `benchmarks/studies/` before the commit (a flagged file stops the commit: report the file to the owner, never hand-edit a run record, RF-73). T260 to T263 land as **one commit ("the pane study")** on `main`: the packed study folders, `noise-floor.json` and the regenerated `docs/llm-efficiency-options.md`. The commit is made on the pinned checkout (T260), then rebased as that single commit onto `main`'s tip, whose commits since T252's must touch only documents and `tasks.md` (anything else: stop and ask the owner); a conflict in `docs/llm-efficiency-options.md` is resolved by regenerating it with T262's command, never by hand-editing it; on the rebased tree T262's compare with `--check` exits 0 and `test_committable_paths.py` passes; then it is pushed. Acceptance: the commit holds exactly those files.

## Dependencies & Execution Order

### Phase dependencies

- **Setup** (T001 to T003): T001, the baseline, runs first, on the worktree at `be01974` before any change; T002 and T003 are documents and follow it.
- **Foundational** (T004 to T019): after T001. Eight test-then-implementation pairs in lanes S, P and G, independent of each other, none moving what a command writes but 34A's attention record, LF on Windows (T010, T011, after T009). Each blocks only the stories that read it: US1 needs T005 and T007; US2 T005, T007, T009 and T017; US3 T009, T013 and T015; US4 T009, T013 and T017; US5 T005, T009, T017 and T019; US6 T009, T013, T015 and T017; US7 T005, T009, T013 and T017; US8 T005 and T017. T008 to T019 may therefore land after US1's first merge, each before its first reader; T010 and T011, which no story reads, before slice 2's gate (T072).
- **US1, slice 1** (T020 to T041): after T005 and T007. The MVP. Gate T041.
- **US2, slice 2** (T042 to T072): first merge after T041, so the 19A and origin-marks commits are on `main` before D12 regenerates the replay fixtures (RF-27, RF-28); also needs T009, T017, T037 and T038. Gate T072.
- **US3 and US4, slice 3** (T073 to T102): first merge after T072. US3 (T073 to T087, lanes S and A) and US4 (T088 to T101, lanes A and S) share the slice and its gate, T102; US4's driver (T098, T099) needs US3's question module (T082) and version-3 scorer (T087).
- **US5, slice 4** (T103 to T158): after T102 (T103, done, records 30A's answer to owner question 8). T126's `stamps.CURRENT` needs T058's `.gitattributes` with `test_line_endings.py` green on the working copy. Gate T158.
- **US6, slice 5** (T159 to T170): after T158. Gate T170, which records R-18 before any paid run (RK-11).
- **US7, slice 6** (T171 to T221): after T170. Gate T220, then the seat's Task Pane smoke review, T221 (33A). **No paid run happens before T220 is ticked** (FR-036) **and T221 has passed** (33A).
- **US8, slice 7** (T222 to T236): after T220. Gate T236, with S-10's printout kept by T235 before it.
- **US5 sizing, slice 8** (T237 to T253): after T236. The offline half (T237 to T242) and its gate, T243, first; then, on a machine with a key and after T221, the smoke run P0 (T244, 35A), then the paid pilot (T245), from the commit of the P0 that passed, and its measurement (T246), the owner's approval (T247), the growth units only when the approved `growth_units` is above 0 (T248 to T251; RF-86 keeps the scoring-rules version), and the pack (T252), all one commit before any study run. Gate T253.
- **Polish** (T254 to T259): after T253; T257 after T254 to T256. Independent of Phase W. T259 runs on the machine holding the owner's identifier lists.
- **Phase W, slice 9** (T260 to T266): after T253, on a machine with a key, every study run from T252's commit with a clean tracked tree (RK-15); T261 to T263 after the last repetition is scored; the study's commit (T260 to T263) before its gate, T264; the private slice (T265) outside the repository, then T266.
- Unlike the template's independent stories, plan.md's delivery order serializes the merges: each slice's gate passes on `main` before the next slice's first merge. A later slice's failing tests may be drafted in its lane early, but nothing of it merges before the gate ahead of it.

## Implementation Strategy

### MVP first (User Story 1 only)

1. Setup: T001's baseline, T002's amendment notes, T003's links.
2. Foundational, only what US1 reads: T004 to T007 (the digests and the system prompt file named beside the checklist). T008 to T019 wait for their first reader; T010 and T011 for slice 2's gate at the latest.
3. Phase 3, US1: T020 to T040 in its commit order, 19A before origin marks.
4. **Stop and validate** at T041: offline, both committed keys load in format 2; the oracle detects every known defect and scores zero on every column it grades; the null review detects nothing; no finding credits a sibling rule or another joint, fit, pair or stack; an old scorecard meets the re-score line. This is the scorer fix every later figure is scored by.

### Incremental delivery

Slice by slice, each closed by its gate on `main`. The owner may stop after any slice: each leaves `main` green and what it delivered usable on its own.

1. Slice 1, US1 (T041): the scorer credits only what was found.
2. Slice 2, US2 (T072): a benchmark run starts reviews as the pane does; provenance schema 2, location-free saved sets, the ledger at 1.1.
3. Slice 3, US3 and US4 (T102): forbidden entries, coverage and questions graded; scripted answers played through the pane's answer path.
4. Slice 4, US5 (T158): owner question 8 answered (30A, T103); the generated committed set, its validation, the golden scorecards and the version record.
5. Slice 5, US6 (T170): the offline report and the prompts record.
6. Slice 6, US7 (T220): attempts that fail say so, resume, cost and packed streams, packages at once (36A) and the live attempt test (35A); then the seat's Task Pane smoke review (T221, 33A), which every paid run waits for. The last slice before money can be spent.
7. Slice 7, US8 (T236): the verdict and the simulator. Stopping here leaves a complete offline eval that can size and judge a study nobody has paid for.
8. Slice 8, US5's sizing (T253): the smoke run P0 (35A), the paid pilot, the cost gate with the study's projected wall clock (36A) and the owner's approval. Stopping here costs P0's four reviews and one three-repetition pilot and leaves the approved size on record.
9. Slice 9, Phase W (T264, T266): the paid pane study's verdict and the private slice.

Polish (T254 to T259) follows T253 whether or not slice 9 runs. If the owner stops before slice 8, the Polish tasks run over the slices that landed, their lists shortened to them and T258's check in the header's short form.

### Parallel team strategy

Within a slice, the lanes it names run beside each other (Phase 2: S, P and G; slice 3: S and A; slice 6: B and R; slice 8: V and G); across slices nothing merges out of order. A lane waiting on a gate drafts its next slice's failing tests on a branch.

**Gaps**: none. Every FR-001 to FR-061 and SC-001 to SC-014 has a failing-first test task and an implementing task. Each row lists every task whose own text cites the requirement, and every task that writes, or turns green, a test of an acceptance scenario the requirement brackets in spec.md; a task that only keeps such a test green (its Acceptance naming it "green" or "green unchanged" as a regression suite) is not listed. SC-012, like FR-047 (offline, no key, no licence), has one dedicated failing-first test, T196's run without the tokenizer vocabulary; otherwise both hold by construction, since every gate runs the suite with `-m "not live"` and no key, and T257 runs the quickstart's offline scenarios on a machine with none. The checks owner decision 33A adds, the add-in's tests and the pane replay at T041, T072 and T220 and the seat's smoke review T221, hold a plan constraint (the pane's behaviour unchanged, plan.md Technical Context) rather than a requirement, so they sit in no row; 34A's migration (T010, T011) likewise.

## Notes

- Task ids are in execution order: phase by phase, then in each phase's order. A task naming another task by id means that task in this list.
- Test ids inside task text are contract test names, not tasks: V-1 to V-27, C-1 to C-17, S-1 to S-10, P-1 to P-11, P-13 and P-14 (`contracts/verdict.md` 12, P-13 and P-14 added for owner decisions 36A and 35A; P-12 is this list's own row, T239, pinning section 10.2's remaining estimators), R-1 to R-19 (`contracts/report.md` 9). Key and case-file entry ids (`D-1`, `C-1`, `FB-1`, `CV-1`, `U-1`, `Q-1`, `A-1`) and request ids (`ER-002`) are data, not tasks.
- "Amends NNN" means the task writes that contract's amendment text in the same commit as the code; the one-line note pointing here is T002's, and T254 confirms each landed.
- Phase 11 carries `[US5]`: the spec maps FR-055 to US5-13 and US5-14 and SC-005, not to US8. Phase W carries no story label; it needs a provider key, never the seat. T221, the one `[W]` task, carries none either: it needs the licensed seat and the key the pane stores (33A).
- From T263 on, the ledger check names the recorded measurement and noise-floor record (the Gates paragraph above); before it the short form is complete.
- Brought forward: P-8's `runs_digest` rows (line endings and key order, RF-56) land in slice 7 (T230), because compare reads `runs_digest` there (C-17); P-8's command half stays in slice 8 (T241).
- S-10's printout: T235 keeps it as `specs/014-design-review-eval/simulation-sweep.txt`; the plan says only "kept with the slice", and the owner may move it.
- Points once left to the owner, now settled: owner question 8 by 30A (T103, done); a keyed set given `--answer-keys` too is refused with one line (RF-85; T120, T121); growth after the pilot keeps the scoring-rules version, its record row declaring `cases_added` (RF-86; T248 to T251, T249's test first). RF-85 and RF-86 are reversible defaults (research §9.13). Nothing happens when `growth_units` is 0.
- Contract gaps Phase 9 resolves (29A), for the owner's review: `AttemptRequest` gains `listener` and `run_dir` (T198); a Gemini transport error exhausting its retries is a `GeminiProviderError` (T177); a package with no run-status entry or no outcome is refused naming resume (T209); a listener error is recorded as `internal_error` and stops the attempt (T197); the price row does not apply to the scripted provider (T202).
- Owner decisions 33A to 36A (2026-10-06, the owner's review of the plan in CLAUDE.md's SMALL CHANGE form, 31A and 32A) added seven tasks, T010 and T011 (34A), T171 (35A's live test), T205 and T206 (36A's `--jobs`), T221 (33A's seat smoke review) and T244 (35A's P0), and edited the gates T041, T072 and T220 (33A) and the pilot's tasks T228, T229, T239, T240 and T245 to T253 (35A, 36A); every later task was renumbered by script and every reference moved with it.
- No task reads a recorded package, run folder, real profile, private key or private case file; SOLIDWORKS is never started by the eval or its tests (T221, on the seat, is a Task Pane review, which reads its documents and writes none); every fixture and case is fictional; nothing committed names a company, a real design or its number, a vault path, a person or a user name (FR-048, FR-051).
